    ✅ SRS purpose\ ✅ BRD vs FRD vs SRS\ ✅ SRS structure\ ✅ Functional specifications\ ✅ Non-functional requirements introduction\ ✅ Interface requirements\ ✅ Data requirements\ ✅ Healthcare AI SRS example

    PHASE 5 -- Requirements Engineering Masterclass

    Lesson 24 -- Advanced User Stories Masterclass

    Writing Industry-Level Agile Requirements as a BA/Product Analyst

    Module Progress Reminder

    Your roadmap:

    Total Lessons: 36

    Completed:

    ✅ Lesson 1--23

    Current:

    ➡️ Lesson 24 of 36

    Remaining:

    12 Lessons Left

    1. What is a User Story?

    Definition

    A user story is a short description of a product requirement written from the perspective of the user, explaining who needs something, what they need, and why they need it.

    Standard format:

    As a \<user role>

    I want \<goal/action>

    So that \<business value>

    Example:

    As a safety reviewer,\ I want AI-generated case summaries,\ so that I can review adverse event reports faster.

    2. Why Do We Use User Stories?

    Traditional documents:

    BRD → FRD → SRS

    are useful for enterprise projects.

    Agile teams need smaller, actionable requirements.

    User stories help:

    ✅ Developers understand user needs\ ✅ Product owners prioritize work\ ✅ QA create tests\ ✅ Teams deliver incremental value

    3. User Story vs Requirement

    Important BA concept.

    A requirement:

    The system shall generate risk scores.

    A user story:

    As a safety reviewer, I want to see AI risk scores so that I can prioritize high-risk cases.

    Difference:

    Requirement User Story

    System-focused User-focused

    Describes functionality Describes user value

    Traditional documentation Agile backlog item

    4. User Story Hierarchy

    Large products use hierarchy.

    Epic

    ↓

    Feature

    ↓

    User Story

    ↓

    Task

    Example: AI Pharmacovigilance Platform

    Epic:

    AI-Assisted Case Review

    Feature:

    AI Risk Assessment

    User Stories:

    Story 1:

    As a reviewer, I want AI risk scores so that I can prioritize cases.

    Story 2:

    As a reviewer, I want risk explanations so that I can trust AI recommendations.

    Story 3:

    As a medical reviewer, I want approval workflow so that high-risk cases receive validation.

    5. Components of a Good User Story

    A professional user story contains:

    1. User Role

    Who needs it?

    Example:

    Safety Reviewer

    2. Goal

    What does the user want?

    Example:

    View AI risk score

    3. Business Value

    Why does it matter?

    Example:

    Prioritize important cases quickly.

    4. Acceptance Criteria

    How do we know it is complete?

    Example:

    Given:

    Case has been analyzed

    When:

    Reviewer opens case

    Then:

    Risk score is displayed.

    6. INVEST Principle

    A good user story follows INVEST.

    I -- Independent

    Should be developed separately.

    Bad:

    \"Login and dashboard and reporting.\"

    Too large.

    Better:

    Story 1:

    Login functionality.

    Story 2:

    Dashboard access.

    Story 3:

    Report generation.

    N -- Negotiable

    Details can be discussed.

    A story is not a fixed technical design.

    V -- Valuable

    Must provide user/business value.

    Bad:

    \"As a user, I want a database.\"

    No user value.

    Good:

    \"As a reviewer, I want previous case history so that I can make better decisions.\"

    E -- Estimable

    Team should estimate effort.

    S -- Small

    Should fit into a sprint.

    T -- Testable

    Must have clear validation.

    7. Writing Good User Stories

    Weak:

    As a user, I want a dashboard.

    Problem:

    • Which user?

    • Why?

    • What information?

    Better:

    As a safety manager, I want a case monitoring dashboard so that I can track pending high-risk cases.

    8. User Story Examples -- Healthcare AI

    Story 1: AI Summary

    User Story

    As a safety reviewer,

    I want AI-generated case summaries,

    so that I can understand reports quickly.

    Acceptance Criteria

    Given:

    A safety report is uploaded.

    When:

    AI processing completes.

    Then:

    The system displays a summary containing:

    • Patient information

    • Drug information

    • Event details

    Story 2: Risk Classification

    User Story

    As a reviewer,

    I want AI risk classification,

    so that I can prioritize serious cases.

    Acceptance Criteria:

    Given:

    A case is analyzed.

    When:

    Risk assessment completes.

    Then:

    System displays:

    • Risk category

    • Confidence score

    • Explanation

    Story 3: Audit Trail

    User Story

    As a compliance officer,

    I want activity history,

    so that I can verify regulatory compliance.

    Acceptance Criteria:

    System records:

    • User action

    • Timestamp

    • Change history

    9. Advanced User Story Template Used in Industry

    Story ID:

    Title:

    Epic:

    User Story:

    As a...

    I want...

    So that...

    Business Value:

    Priority:

    Dependencies:

    Assumptions:

    Acceptance Criteria:

    Definition of Done:

    Example:

    Story ID:

    PV-AI-001

    Title:

    AI Case Summary Generation

    Epic:

    AI Case Processing

    User Story:

    As a safety reviewer,

    I want AI-generated summaries,

    so that I can reduce manual reading effort.

    Priority:

    High

    Acceptance Criteria:

    1. User uploads report.

    2. AI processes document.

    3. Summary is generated.

    4. Reviewer can edit summary.

    10. User Story Splitting Techniques

    Large stories should be split.

    Example:

    Large Story:

    \"As a reviewer, I want complete AI case management.\"

    Too large.

    Split:

    1. Upload case document.

    2. Extract information.

    3. Generate summary.

    4. Display risk score.

    5. Approve case.

    Techniques:

    Split by Workflow

    Example:

    Create → Review → Approve

    Split by User Role

    Example:

    Reviewer vs Manager

    Split by Data Type

    Example:

    Patient data vs Drug data

    11. User Story vs Use Case

    Another interview topic.

    User Story Use Case

    Agile requirement Detailed interaction

    Short Detailed

    Sprint planning System analysis

    User value focused Actor-system behavior

    Example:

    User Story:

    \"Reviewer wants risk score.\"

    Use Case:

    Detailed steps of how system generates and displays risk score.

    12. Common BA Mistakes

    Mistake 1:

    Writing technical stories.

    Wrong:

    \"As a developer, I want database optimization.\"

    Mistake 2:

    No business value.

    Mistake 3:

    Too large stories.

    Mistake 4:

    Missing acceptance criteria.

    13. Interview Questions

    Q1. What is a user story?

    Answer:

    \"A user story is an Agile requirement describing user needs, desired functionality, and business value.\"

    Q2. What is INVEST?

    Answer:

    \"INVEST defines characteristics of good user stories: Independent, Negotiable, Valuable, Estimable, Small, and Testable.\"

    Q3. Difference between Epic and User Story?

    Answer:

    \"An Epic is a large business capability that contains multiple smaller user stories deliverable within sprints.\"

    Practical Assignment

    Create User Stories for:

    AI Pharmacovigilance Platform

    Create:

    Epic:

    AI Case Review

    Create 5 user stories:

    For each:

    1. User role

    2. Goal

    3. Business value

    4. Acceptance criteria

    Example:

    \"As a medical reviewer, I want approval workflow so that high-risk cases are validated before submission.\"

    PHASE 5 -- Requirements Engineering Masterclass

    Lesson 25 -- Acceptance Criteria Masterclass

    Converting User Expectations into Clear, Testable Conditions

    Module Progress Reminder

    Your roadmap:

    Total Lessons: 36

    Completed:

    ✅ Lesson 1--24

    Current:

    ➡️ Lesson 25 of 36

    Remaining:

    11 Lessons Left

    1. What are Acceptance Criteria?

    Definition

    Acceptance Criteria are a set of conditions that define when a user story or requirement is considered complete and acceptable by the business.

    Simple:

    Acceptance Criteria answer:

    \"How will we know this feature is working correctly?\"

    Example:

    User Story:

    As a safety reviewer, I want AI-generated summaries so that I can review cases faster.

    Without Acceptance Criteria:

    Developer may create any type of summary.

    With Acceptance Criteria:

    The team knows exactly what is expected.

    2. Why are Acceptance Criteria Important?

    Acceptance criteria help:

    ✅ Remove ambiguity\ ✅ Align BA, Product Owner, Developer, QA\ ✅ Create test cases\ ✅ Define completion\ ✅ Reduce disputes

    Example:

    Requirement:

    \"System should send notifications.\"

    Questions:

    • When?

    • To whom?

    • What type?

    • What happens if delivery fails?

    Acceptance criteria clarify these.

    3. Acceptance Criteria in Agile Flow

    Business Need

    ↓

    User Story

    ↓

    Acceptance Criteria

    ↓

    Development

    ↓

    Testing

    ↓

    Acceptance

    ↓

    Release

    4. Who Creates Acceptance Criteria?

    Usually:

    • Business Analyst

    • Product Owner

    • Business Stakeholders

    • QA Team

    Collaboration:

    BA:

    Defines business expectation.

    Developer:

    Checks feasibility.

    QA:

    Creates test scenarios.

    5. Types of Acceptance Criteria

    There are three major types.

    Type 1: Scenario-Based Acceptance Criteria

    Most common.

    Uses:

    Given -- When -- Then

    (Gherkin format)

    Structure:

    Given

    Initial condition

    When

    User action

    Then

    Expected result

    Example:

    User Story:

    As a safety reviewer, I want to upload safety reports.

    Acceptance Criteria:

    Given the user is logged in

    When the user uploads a valid PDF report

    Then the system should store the document successfully

    And display confirmation message.

    Type 2: Rule-Based Acceptance Criteria

    Used for business rules.

    Example:

    Requirement:

    High-risk cases require approval.

    Acceptance Criteria:

    Given risk score is above 80%

    When reviewer submits case

    Then medical approval should be required.

    Type 3: Checklist-Based Acceptance Criteria

    Used for simple features.

    Example:

    Login Feature:

    Acceptance:

    ☑ User enters username\ ☑ User enters password\ ☑ Invalid login shows error\ ☑ Successful login opens dashboard

    6. Functional Acceptance Criteria

    Defines system behavior.

    Example:

    User Story:

    AI should generate risk score.

    Acceptance Criteria:

    Given a case has been analyzed

    When reviewer opens case details

    Then risk score should be displayed.

    7. Non-Functional Acceptance Criteria

    Defines quality expectations.

    Examples:

    • Performance

    • Security

    • Availability

    • Usability

    Example:

    Performance:

    Given dashboard has loaded

    When user opens case list

    Then data should display within 3 seconds.

    Security:

    Given user does not have permission

    When accessing restricted case

    Then system should deny access.

    8. Positive and Negative Acceptance Criteria

    A professional BA always covers both.

    Positive Scenario

    Expected behavior.

    Example:

    Valid document upload.

    Given PDF file is valid

    When user uploads

    Then upload succeeds.

    Negative Scenario

    Error handling.

    Example:

    Invalid document.

    Given file format is unsupported

    When user uploads

    Then system displays error message.

    9. Acceptance Criteria Example -- AI Pharmacovigilance Platform

    User Story:

    As a safety reviewer,

    I want AI-generated case summaries,

    so that I can review reports faster.

    Acceptance Criteria:

    AC-001: Generate Summary

    Given a safety report is uploaded

    When AI analysis completes

    Then the system should generate a summary.

    AC-002: Display Important Information

    Given summary is generated

    When reviewer opens case

    Then system should display:

    - Drug information

    - Patient details

    - Adverse event information

    AC-003: AI Explanation

    Given AI provides risk classification

    When reviewer views assessment

    Then explanation should be available.

    AC-004: Processing Failure

    Given AI processing fails

    When reviewer opens case

    Then system should show failure status and retry option.

    10. Acceptance Criteria vs Definition of Done

    Important Agile interview question.

    Acceptance Criteria

    Specific to a user story.

    Example:

    \"Risk score should be displayed.\"

    Definition of Done

    Applies to all work.

    Example:

    • Code reviewed

    • Testing completed

    • Documentation updated

    • Product owner approval received

    Comparison:

    Acceptance Criteria Definition of Done

    Feature-specific Overall completion standard

    Story level Sprint level

    Written by BA/PO Team agreement

    11. Writing Good Acceptance Criteria

    Rule 1:

    Must be testable.

    Bad:

    \"System should be user-friendly.\"

    Good:

    \"User should complete case submission within 5 steps.\"

    Rule 2:

    Avoid technical implementation.

    Bad:

    \"Use React component.\"

    Good:

    \"Dashboard should display case status.\"

    Rule 3:

    Cover edge cases.

    Example:

    What if:

    • Data missing?

    • AI fails?

    • User unauthorized?

    Rule 4:

    Keep language simple.

    12. Common BA Mistakes

    Mistake 1:

    Writing acceptance criteria after development starts.

    Mistake 2:

    Making criteria too broad.

    Mistake 3:

    Ignoring negative scenarios.

    Mistake 4:

    Writing technical solutions.

    13. BA + QA Collaboration

    Acceptance criteria become test cases.

    Example:

    Acceptance Criteria:

    \"System should block invalid files.\"

    ↓

    QA Test Case:

    Step Expected Result

    Upload invalid file Error shown

    14. Interview Questions

    Q1. What are acceptance criteria?

    Answer:

    \"Acceptance criteria define the conditions that a product increment must satisfy before a user story is accepted.\"

    Q2. Why use Given-When-Then?

    Answer:

    \"Given-When-Then provides a structured way to describe scenarios clearly and allows easy conversion into test cases.\"

    Q3. Difference between requirement and acceptance criteria?

    Answer:

    \"Requirement explains what needs to be built, while acceptance criteria define how we verify that it has been built correctly.\"

    Practical Assignment

    Create acceptance criteria for:

    User Story:

    \"As a medical reviewer, I want to approve high-risk cases so that regulatory decisions are properly validated.\"

    Create:

    1. Positive scenario

    2. Negative scenario

    3. Business rule scenario

    4. Security scenario

    Lesson Summary

    Today you learned:

    ✅ Acceptance criteria concept\ ✅ Given-When-Then format\ ✅ Functional criteria\ ✅ Non-functional criteria\ ✅ Positive/negative scenarios\ ✅ BA-QA collaboration\ ✅ Healthcare AI examples

    PHASE 5 -- Requirements Engineering Masterclass

    Lesson 26 -- Non-Functional Requirements (NFR) Masterclass

    Defining Quality, Performance, Security & Reliability Expectations

    Module Progress Reminder

    Your roadmap:

    Total Lessons: 36

    Completed:

    ✅ Lesson 1--25

    Current:

    ➡️ Lesson 26 of 36

    Remaining:

    10 Lessons Left

    1. What are Non-Functional Requirements (NFR)?

    Definition

    Non-Functional Requirements define the quality attributes, performance expectations, security standards, and operational characteristics of a system.

    Simple:

    Functional Requirement:

    What the system does.

    Non-Functional Requirement:

    How well the system should do it.

    Example:

    Functional Requirement:

    \"The system shall generate AI risk scores.\"

    Non-Functional Requirement:

    \"The system shall generate risk scores within 30 seconds with 99% availability.\"

    2. Functional vs Non-Functional Requirements

    Functional Requirement Non-Functional Requirement

    System behavior System quality

    Features Performance & constraints

    User actions User experience

    What How well

    Example:

    Functional:

    User can upload adverse event reports.

    Non-Functional:

    System should support 10,000 simultaneous uploads.

    3. Why Are NFRs Important?

    Without NFRs:

    A system may work but fail in real-world usage.

    Example:

    AI system generates correct risk scores.

    But:

    • Takes 10 minutes

    • Crashes frequently

    • Has weak security

    The product fails.

    NFRs ensure:

    ✅ Reliability\ ✅ Security\ ✅ Scalability\ ✅ Better user experience\ ✅ Regulatory compliance

    4. Major Categories of Non-Functional Requirements

    Industry BAs commonly use these categories:

    1. Performance

    2. Security

    3. Availability

    4. Reliability

    5. Scalability

    6. Usability

    7. Maintainability

    8. Compatibility

    9. Compliance

    10. Auditability

    5. Performance Requirements

    Definition:

    Defines how fast and efficiently the system responds.

    Examples:

    Response Time

    \"The dashboard shall load within 3 seconds.\"

    Processing Time

    \"AI document analysis shall complete within 60 seconds.\"

    Throughput

    \"The system shall process 5,000 reports per hour.\"

    Healthcare AI Example:

    Bad:

    \"AI should be fast.\"

    Good:

    \"The AI engine shall generate case summaries within 30 seconds for documents up to 20 MB.\"

    6. Security Requirements

    Very important in healthcare.

    Security covers:

    • Authentication

    • Authorization

    • Data protection

    • Encryption

    • Access control

    Examples:

    Authentication

    \"Users shall authenticate using multi-factor authentication.\"

    Authorization

    \"Only medical reviewers can approve high-risk cases.\"

    Encryption

    \"Patient data shall be encrypted during storage and transmission.\"

    Healthcare Example:

    A safety reviewer should not access another company\'s patient data.

    Requirement:

    \"System shall enforce organization-level data isolation.\"

    7. Availability Requirements

    Definition:

    How often the system should be operational.

    Example:

    \"The platform shall maintain 99.5% availability.\"

    Calculation:

    99.5% availability means approximately:

    \~3.65 hours downtime per month.

    Healthcare Example:

    Regulatory reporting systems require high availability.

    8. Reliability Requirements

    Definition:

    Ability of the system to operate consistently without failure.

    Examples:

    \"The system shall recover failed AI processing automatically.\"

    \"The system shall not lose uploaded case information during failures.\"

    Reliability Metrics:

    • Error rate

    • Recovery time

    • Failure frequency

    9. Scalability Requirements

    Definition:

    Ability to handle increasing workload.

    Example:

    Today:

    10,000 reports/month

    Future:

    100,000 reports/month

    System should support growth.

    Requirement:

    \"The system shall support increasing case volume without performance degradation.\"

    10. Usability Requirements

    Focus:

    User experience.

    Examples:

    \"The reviewer shall complete case approval within five steps.\"

    \"The system shall provide clear error messages.\"

    \"The dashboard shall support users with limited technical knowledge.\"

    11. Maintainability Requirements

    Focus:

    Ease of modification and support.

    Examples:

    • Code should be modular.

    • System should support future AI model updates.

    • Changes should not impact existing functionality.

    12. Compatibility Requirements

    Defines supported environments.

    Examples:

    \"The application shall support Chrome, Edge, and Firefox browsers.\"

    \"The mobile application shall support Android and iOS.\"

    13. Compliance Requirements

    Critical for healthcare products.

    Examples:

    • Data privacy

    • Regulatory reporting

    • Audit requirements

    Healthcare AI Example:

    \"The system shall maintain complete audit history of AI-generated recommendations.\"

    14. Auditability Requirements

    Very important in regulated industries.

    System should record:

    • Who performed action

    • What changed

    • When it changed

    • Previous value

    • New value

    Example:

    Before:

    Risk Score = Medium

    After:

    Risk Score = High

    System records:

    User:\ Dr. Smith

    Time:\ 10:30 AM

    Reason:\ Updated medical assessment

    15. AI-Specific Non-Functional Requirements

    For AI products, additional NFRs are needed.

    1. AI Accuracy

    Example:

    \"AI classification accuracy should be monitored continuously.\"

    2. Explainability

    Example:

    \"System shall provide reasons behind AI recommendations.\"

    3. Bias Monitoring

    Example:

    \"AI model performance shall be evaluated across different datasets.\"

    4. Human Oversight

    Example:

    \"Critical safety decisions require human approval.\"

    5. Model Performance Monitoring

    Example:

    \"System shall track AI prediction confidence scores.\"

    16. Writing Professional NFRs

    Use:

    \"The system shall...\"

    Bad:

    \"The system should be secure.\"

    Good:

    \"The system shall encrypt sensitive data using approved encryption standards.\"

    Bad:

    \"The system should be scalable.\"

    Good:

    \"The system shall support 100,000 monthly cases without degradation.\"

    17. Complete Healthcare AI NFR Example

    Product:

    AI Pharmacovigilance Platform

    Performance

    NFR-001:

    System shall generate AI summaries within 30 seconds.

    Security

    NFR-002:

    System shall encrypt patient-related information.

    Availability

    NFR-003:

    System shall maintain 99.5% uptime.

    Scalability

    NFR-004:

    System shall support 10 times current case volume.

    Audit

    NFR-005:

    System shall maintain complete user activity logs.

    Explainability

    NFR-006:

    System shall display AI reasoning for every risk prediction.

    18. NFR Traceability

    Professional BA connects:

    Business Need

    ↓

    NFR

    ↓

    User Story

    ↓

    Acceptance Criteria

    ↓

    Test Case

    Example:

    Business Need:

    Protect patient data.

    ↓

    NFR:

    Encrypt patient information.

    ↓

    Acceptance Criteria:

    Given sensitive data is stored,

    Then data must be encrypted.

    19. Common BA Mistakes

    Mistake 1:

    Ignoring NFRs.

    Mistake 2:

    Using vague words:

    • Fast

    • Secure

    • Easy

    Mistake 3:

    Not making NFR measurable.

    Mistake 4:

    Missing compliance requirements.

    20. Interview Questions

    Q1. What are NFRs?

    Answer:

    \"Non-functional requirements define system quality attributes such as performance, security, scalability, reliability, and usability.\"

    Q2. Give examples of NFRs.

    Answer:

    \"Response time, availability, security, scalability, auditability, and compliance requirements are examples of NFRs.\"

    Q3. Why are NFRs important in healthcare systems?

    Answer:

    \"Healthcare systems handle sensitive data and require high reliability, security, compliance, and auditability.\"

    Practical Assignment

    Create NFRs for:

    AI Pharmacovigilance Platform

    Write minimum:

    Performance:

    2 requirements

    Security:

    2 requirements

    Availability:

    1 requirement

    Scalability:

    1 requirement

    AI Explainability:

    1 requirement

    Compliance:

    1 requirement

    Today you learned:

    ✅ Functional vs Non-functional requirements\ ✅ Performance requirements\ ✅ Security requirements\ ✅ Availability & reliability\ ✅ Scalability\ ✅ Usability\ ✅ Compliance\ ✅ AI-specific NFRs\ ✅ Healthcare AI examples

    Lesson 27 -- BA Role in Scrum Masterclass

    How a Business Analyst Works Inside an Agile Scrum Team

    Module Progress Reminder

    Your roadmap:

    Total Lessons: 36

    1. Why Does a BA Need to Understand Scrum?

    Modern organizations rarely use only traditional waterfall projects.

    Most companies use:

    • Scrum

    • SAFe

    • Hybrid Agile

    • Product-based delivery

    A BA must understand:

    • How requirements flow

    • How teams collaborate

    • How value is delivered

    2. Scrum Team Structure

    A Scrum Team has three main accountabilities:

    Scrum Team

    |

    --------------------------------

    | | |

    Product Owner Scrum Master Developers

    Where does BA fit?

    A BA can work in different ways:

    Model 1:

    BA supports Product Owner

    (Common in enterprise)

    Model 2:

    BA works as part of delivery team

    Model 3:

    BA performs Product Analyst role

    3. BA vs Product Owner vs Scrum Master

    Very important interview topic.

    Role Main Focus

    Product Owner What and Why to build

    Scrum Master How team follows Scrum

    BA Analyze needs and translate them into requirements

    Developers Build solution

    Example:

    Feature:

    AI Risk Assessment Dashboard

    Product Owner:

    \"Users need AI risk visibility.\"

    Defines:

    • Priority

    • Business value

    BA:

    Analyzes:

    • User needs

    • Workflow

    • Requirements

    • Acceptance criteria

    Scrum Master:

    Ensures:

    • Scrum events happen

    • Team collaboration

    Developers:

    Build dashboard.

    4. BA Activities Before Sprint

    A BA\'s work starts before development.

    Activity 1: Requirement Discovery

    BA understands:

    • Business problem

    • User needs

    • Stakeholder expectations

    Example:

    Problem:

    Safety reviewers cannot prioritize cases.

    Activity 2: Requirement Analysis

    BA converts:

    Business need →

    Feature →

    User Story

    Example:

    Epic:

    AI Case Review

    ↓

    Feature:

    Risk Classification

    ↓

    Story:

    \"As a reviewer, I want risk scores...\"

    Activity 3: Backlog Preparation

    BA helps create:

    • User stories

    • Acceptance criteria

    • Dependencies

    • Business rules

    Activity 4: Backlog Refinement Support

    BA ensures stories are:

    ✅ Clear\ ✅ Complete\ ✅ Understandable

    5. BA Role During Sprint

    During sprint execution, BA continues collaboration.

    1. Clarifying Requirements

    Developer asks:

    \"What happens if AI confidence is low?\"

    BA explains:

    \"Cases below 70% confidence require manual review.\"

    2. Supporting Developers

    BA helps with:

    • Business rules

    • User workflows

    • Edge cases

    3. Supporting QA

    BA helps create:

    • Test scenarios

    • Expected behavior

    Example:

    Acceptance Criteria:

    \"If risk score >80%, approval required.\"

    QA creates test:

    Input:

    Risk score 85%

    Expected:

    Approval workflow appears.

    4. Reviewing Completed Features

    BA checks:

    Does feature solve user problem?

    Example:

    Developer built dashboard.

    BA checks:

    • Are high-risk cases visible?

    • Is explanation shown?

    • Can reviewer complete task?

    6. BA Role in Scrum Ceremonies

    A BA participates in all important Scrum events.

    1. Sprint Planning

    BA helps:

    • Explain requirements

    • Clarify acceptance criteria

    • Discuss dependencies

    Example:

    Story:

    AI Summary Feature

    BA explains:

    \"The summary must include drug, event, and severity information.\"

    2. Daily Scrum

    Usually developers lead.

    BA may join to:

    • Answer questions

    • Remove requirement confusion

    3. Sprint Review

    BA helps demonstrate:

    • Completed functionality

    • Business value

    Example:

    \"Reviewer can now identify high-risk cases faster.\"

    4. Sprint Retrospective

    BA contributes improvement ideas.

    Example:

    \"Acceptance criteria should be created earlier.\"

    7. BA and Product Backlog

    A BA does not usually own the backlog, but supports backlog quality.

    Product Owner owns:

    • Priority

    • Business decisions

    BA supports:

    • Requirement clarity

    • Story refinement

    • Analysis

    Good backlog item:

    Epic

    ↓

    Feature

    ↓

    User Story

    ↓

    Acceptance Criteria

    ↓

    Tasks

    8. BA Role in User Story Lifecycle

    Lifecycle:

    Idea

    ↓

    Analyze

    ↓

    Write Story

    ↓

    Add Acceptance Criteria

    ↓

    Refine

    ↓

    Develop

    ↓

    Test

    ↓

    Accept

    BA involvement:

    Almost every stage.

    9. Healthcare AI Scrum Example

    Product:

    AI Pharmacovigilance Platform

    Sprint Goal:

    Reduce manual case review effort.

    Sprint Stories:

    Story 1:

    Upload safety report.

    Story 2:

    Extract drug information.

    Story 3:

    Generate AI summary.

    Story 4:

    Display risk score.

    BA Activities:

    Before Sprint:

    • Interview reviewers

    • Define stories

    • Create acceptance criteria

    During Sprint:

    • Clarify AI workflow

    • Support developers

    After Sprint:

    • Validate feature with users

    10. BA Deliverables in Agile

    A BA may create:

    Before Sprint:

    • User stories

    • Acceptance criteria

    • Process diagrams

    • Requirement notes

    During Sprint:

    • Clarifications

    • Updated requirements

    • Decision logs

    After Sprint:

    • UAT support

    • Release notes

    • Documentation

    11. Common BA Mistakes in Scrum

    Mistake 1:

    Thinking BA disappears in Agile.

    Wrong.

    Agile needs analysis continuously.

    Mistake 2:

    Writing stories without user value.

    Mistake 3:

    Creating unclear acceptance criteria.

    Mistake 4:

    Not collaborating with developers.

    12. Interview Questions

    Q1. What is the role of BA in Scrum?

    Answer:

    \"A BA helps understand business needs, analyze requirements, create user stories, define acceptance criteria, and collaborate with stakeholders and delivery teams.\"

    Q2. Does Scrum have a BA role?

    Answer:

    \"Scrum does not officially define a BA role, but organizations often use BAs to support Product Owners and development teams.\"

    Q3. Difference between BA and Product Owner?

    Answer:

    \"Product Owner focuses on product vision and prioritization, while BA focuses on detailed analysis and translating needs into actionable requirements.\"

    Lesson 28 -- Backlog Refinement Masterclass

    How a BA Converts Ideas into Development-Ready User Stories

    Module Progress Reminder

    Your roadmap:

    Total Lessons: 36

    Completed:

    ✅ Lesson 1--27

    Current:

    ➡️ Lesson 28 of 36

    Remaining:

    8 Lessons Left

    1. What is Backlog Refinement?

    Definition

    Backlog Refinement (also called Backlog Grooming) is the Agile activity where the Scrum team reviews, analyzes, clarifies, prioritizes, and prepares upcoming backlog items before they enter a sprint.

    Simple:

    Backlog refinement means:

    Making sure the team is ready before development starts.

    Without refinement:

    Developer:

    \"What exactly should I build?\"

    QA:

    \"How do I test this?\"

    Product Owner:

    \"I thought it meant something else.\"

    With refinement:

    Everyone understands:

    • What to build

    • Why to build

    • How to validate

    2. Why is Backlog Refinement Important?

    A good refinement process ensures:

    ✅ Clear requirements\ ✅ Smaller stories\ ✅ Better estimates\ ✅ Fewer sprint blockers\ ✅ Better collaboration\ ✅ Faster delivery

    3. Who Participates in Backlog Refinement?

    Typical participants:

    Role Contribution

    Product Owner Business priority

    BA Requirement clarity

    Developers Technical feasibility

    QA Testability

    UX Designer Design requirements

    Scrum Master Facilitation

    4. BA Role in Backlog Refinement

    The BA is one of the most important contributors.

    BA Responsibility 1:

    Explain Business Context

    Not only:

    \"What to build\"

    but:

    \"Why it matters.\"

    Example:

    Story:

    \"Display AI risk score.\"

    BA explains:

    \"Reviewers currently spend hours identifying critical cases. Risk score helps prioritize.\"

    BA Responsibility 2:

    Clarify User Stories

    BA checks:

    • Is user clear?

    • Is goal clear?

    • Is value clear?

    Bad Story:

    As a user, I want reports.

    Questions:

    Which user?

    What reports?

    Why?

    Improved:

    As a safety manager, I want compliance reports so that I can monitor regulatory submissions.

    BA Responsibility 3:

    Define Acceptance Criteria

    BA ensures:

    Developer knows expected behavior.

    Example:

    Story:

    AI summary generation.

    Acceptance Criteria:

    Given a report is uploaded

    When AI analysis completes

    Then summary should display key safety information.

    BA Responsibility 4:

    Identify Missing Requirements

    BA asks:

    • What happens if data is missing?

    • What happens if AI fails?

    • Who can access this feature?

    • What are business rules?

    5. Backlog Refinement Process

    Typical flow:

    Review Epic

    ↓

    Understand Business Goal

    ↓

    Break into Features

    ↓

    Create User Stories

    ↓

    Split Large Stories

    ↓

    Add Acceptance Criteria

    ↓

    Identify Dependencies

    ↓

    Estimate Effort

    ↓

    Ready for Sprint

    6. Story Readiness Checklist

    Before sprint, a story should be:

    Clear

    Everyone understands it.

    Valuable

    Has business/user value.

    Small

    Can fit inside sprint.

    Testable

    Has acceptance criteria.

    Feasible

    Team can build it.

    7. Definition of Ready (DoR)

    Very important Agile concept.

    Definition:

    Definition of Ready is the checklist that determines whether a backlog item is prepared for development.

    A story is ready when:

    ✅ User story written\ ✅ Acceptance criteria defined\ ✅ Dependencies identified\ ✅ UX requirements available\ ✅ Business rules documented\ ✅ Team understands requirement

    Example:

    Before:

    \"Build AI dashboard.\"

    Not ready.

    After:

    \"Create reviewer dashboard showing pending cases, risk score, and priority.\"

    Ready.

    8. Story Splitting Techniques

    Large stories create problems.

    Example:

    Large Story:

    As a reviewer, I want complete AI case management.

    Too large.

    Split:

    Story 1:

    Upload safety report.

    Story 2:

    Extract report information.

    Story 3:

    Generate AI summary.

    Story 4:

    Display risk score.

    Story 5:

    Approve case.

    9. Backlog Refinement Example -- Healthcare AI

    Epic:

    AI Pharmacovigilance Case Review

    Feature:

    AI Case Prioritization

    Initial Story:

    \"Improve case prioritization.\"

    Too vague.

    BA Refinement:

    User:

    Safety Reviewer

    Goal:

    View priority cases

    Value:

    Review serious cases faster

    Final Story:

    As a safety reviewer, I want AI-generated case priority levels so that I can focus on high-risk adverse events first.

    Acceptance Criteria:

    AC-001

    Given case analysis is completed

    When reviewer opens dashboard

    Then priority level should be displayed.

    AC-002

    High-risk cases should appear at top.

    AC-003

    Reviewer can filter cases by priority.

    10. Dependency Identification

    BA identifies dependencies.

    Example:

    Feature:

    AI Risk Score

    Dependencies:

    • AI model availability

    • Patient data access

    • Drug database integration

    • User permissions

    Why important?

    Avoid sprint delays.

    11. Estimation Readiness

    BA does not estimate technical effort.

    But helps make stories understandable.

    Example:

    Bad:

    \"Create AI system.\"

    Impossible to estimate.

    Good:

    \"Create API to display AI risk score.\"

    Can be estimated.

    12. Refinement Meeting Simulation

    Story:

    AI Summary Generation

    BA:

    \"Reviewer needs automated summary because manual review takes 30 minutes.\"

    Developer:

    \"Which documents are supported?\"

    BA:

    \"PDF and XML initially.\"

    QA:

    \"What happens if extraction fails?\"

    BA:

    \"System should display error and allow retry.\"

    UX:

    \"Where should summary appear?\"

    BA:

    \"Case details page.\"

    Result:

    Ready story.

    13. Common Backlog Refinement Mistakes

    Mistake 1:

    Refining too late.

    Mistake 2:

    Discussing solutions before understanding problem.

    Mistake 3:

    Stories are too big.

    Mistake 4:

    No acceptance criteria.

    Mistake 5:

    Ignoring dependencies.

    14. Interview Questions

    Q1. What is backlog refinement?

    Answer:

    \"Backlog refinement is the process of reviewing and preparing backlog items by clarifying requirements, adding acceptance criteria, splitting stories, and ensuring readiness for future sprints.\"

    Q2. What is Definition of Ready?

    Answer:

    \"Definition of Ready is a checklist that confirms a user story has enough information to enter a sprint.\"

    Q3. What is BA\'s role in backlog refinement?

    Answer:

    \"BA helps clarify requirements, define acceptance criteria, identify gaps, split stories, and ensure the team understands business needs.\"

    Practical Assignment

    For AI Pharmacovigilance Platform:

    Create a refinement session.

    Take this Epic:

    Epic:

    AI-Assisted Safety Review

    Create:

    1. Two Features

    2. Five User Stories

    3. Acceptance Criteria

    4. Dependencies

    5. Definition of Ready checklist

    Today you learned:

    ✅ Backlog refinement concept\ ✅ BA role in refinement\ ✅ Definition of Ready\ ✅ Story splitting\ ✅ Dependency identification\ ✅ Healthcare AI refinement example\ ✅ Refinement meeting process

    Lesson 29 -- Sprint Planning Collaboration Masterclass

    How a BA Supports Sprint Planning and Sprint Execution

    Module Progress Reminder

    Your roadmap:

    Total Lessons: 36

    Completed:

    ✅ Lesson 1--28

    Current:

    ➡️ Lesson 29 of 36

    Remaining:

    7 Lessons Left

    1. What is Sprint Planning?

    Definition

    Sprint Planning is a Scrum event where the team decides what work will be completed during the upcoming sprint and how that work will be achieved.

    Simple:

    Sprint Planning answers two questions:

    Question 1:

    What are we going to build?

    (Product Backlog selection)

    Question 2:

    How will we build it?

    (Technical approach and tasks)

    2. Sprint Planning Inputs

    Before Sprint Planning begins, the team needs:

    ✅ Product Backlog\ ✅ Prioritized User Stories\ ✅ Acceptance Criteria\ ✅ Definition of Ready\ ✅ Product Goal\ ✅ Team Capacity

    This is where BA preparation becomes important.

    3. Sprint Planning Participants

    Scrum Team:

    Role Responsibility

    Product Owner Explains priority and business value

    BA Clarifies requirements and workflows

    Scrum Master Facilitates meeting

    Developers Estimate and plan implementation

    4. BA Role Before Sprint Planning

    A BA prepares the team.

    1. Ensure Stories Are Ready

    BA checks:

    • User story exists

    • Acceptance criteria defined

    • Business rules documented

    • Dependencies identified

    Example:

    Before:

    \"Improve AI review process.\"

    Not ready.

    After:

    \"As a safety reviewer, I want AI risk scores so that I can prioritize serious cases.\"

    Ready.

    2. Validate Business Priority

    BA works with Product Owner.

    Example:

    Priority:

    High:

    AI risk classification

    Medium:

    Dashboard customization

    Low:

    Export formatting

    3. Clarify Dependencies

    Example:

    Story:

    AI risk prediction

    Dependencies:

    • AI model

    • Historical safety data

    • Drug database API

    5. BA Role During Sprint Planning

    Activity 1:

    Explain User Stories

    BA explains:

    • User problem

    • Expected behavior

    • Business value

    Example:

    Story:

    AI Summary Generation

    BA explains:

    \"Reviewers spend 30 minutes reading each report. AI summary should reduce review time.\"

    Activity 2:

    Answer Requirement Questions

    Developer:

    \"Should users edit AI summaries?\"

    BA:

    \"Yes, reviewers can edit before approval.\"

    Activity 3:

    Clarify Acceptance Criteria

    Example:

    Acceptance:

    \"Risk score should be displayed.\"

    Developer asks:

    \"Where?\"

    BA clarifies:

    \"On case details page above medical review section.\"

    Activity 4:

    Identify Missing Scenarios

    BA asks:

    \"What happens when AI confidence is low?\"

    Decision:

    Low confidence cases require manual review.

    6. Sprint Planning Structure

    Typical flow:

    Sprint Planning

    ↓

    Review Product Goal

    ↓

    Select Stories

    ↓

    Discuss Requirements

    ↓

    Estimate Capacity

    ↓

    Create Sprint Goal

    ↓

    Break Stories into Tasks

    7. Sprint Goal

    A sprint should have a clear goal.

    Example:

    Bad:

    \"Complete five stories.\"

    Good:

    \"Enable safety reviewers to automatically analyze and prioritize adverse event cases.\"

    Why?

    It focuses on value, not only tasks.

    8. Story Selection Example

    Product Backlog

    AI Pharmacovigilance Platform

    Top Items:

    Story Priority

    Upload safety reports High

    AI summary generation High

    Dashboard filters Medium

    User profile customization Low

    Sprint Capacity:

    2 weeks

    Team selects:

    1. Upload safety reports

    2. AI summary generation

    3. Dashboard basic view

    9. BA Role in Sprint Scope Discussion

    Sometimes teams cannot complete everything.

    BA helps decide:

    What is essential?

    Example:

    AI Summary Feature:

    Must Have:

    ✅ Generate summary

    Can Wait:

    ❌ Summary formatting options

    10. Sprint Planning Example -- Healthcare AI

    Sprint Goal:

    \"Enable AI-assisted safety case analysis.\"

    Selected Stories:

    Story 1:

    As a reviewer, I want to upload adverse event documents.

    Acceptance:

    • PDF accepted

    • Document stored

    • Case created

    Story 2:

    As a reviewer, I want AI-generated summaries.

    Acceptance:

    • Summary generated

    • Key information displayed

    • Reviewer can edit

    Story 3:

    As a reviewer, I want risk scores.

    Acceptance:

    • Score displayed

    • Category shown

    • Explanation available

    11. BA During Sprint Execution

    Sprint planning is not the end.

    BA continues support.

    Daily Activities:

    • Answer questions

    • Clarify requirements

    • Update stories if needed

    • Coordinate with stakeholders

    Example:

    Developer:

    \"Business wants risk score visible?\"

    BA:

    \"Yes, this is a mandatory acceptance criteria.\"

    12. Sprint Planning Mistakes

    Mistake 1:

    Taking unclear stories into sprint.

    Mistake 2:

    Missing acceptance criteria.

    Mistake 3:

    Ignoring technical dependencies.

    Mistake 4:

    Selecting too much work.

    Mistake 5:

    BA disappears after sprint starts.

    13. Sprint Planning vs Backlog Refinement

    Interview question.

    Backlog Refinement Sprint Planning

    Prepare future work Select current sprint work

    Continuous activity Beginning of sprint

    Clarify stories Commit sprint goal

    Estimate readiness Plan execution

    14. BA Deliverables Related to Sprint Planning

    BA may provide:

    ✅ User stories\ ✅ Acceptance criteria\ ✅ Process flows\ ✅ Business rules\ ✅ Requirement notes\ ✅ Data requirements\ ✅ Decision logs

    15. Interview Questions

    Q1. What is BA\'s role in Sprint Planning?

    Answer:

    \"BA helps ensure selected stories are clear, explains business requirements, clarifies acceptance criteria, identifies dependencies, and supports the team during planning.\"

    Q2. Does BA decide sprint priority?

    Answer:

    \"Usually the Product Owner owns priority, while BA supports with analysis and business understanding.\"

    Q3. What happens if requirements are unclear during Sprint Planning?

    Answer:

    \"The BA clarifies requirements, updates acceptance criteria, or helps the team decide whether the story is ready.\"

    PHASE 6 -- Agile BA Execution

    Lesson 30 -- UAT Planning & Execution Masterclass

    How a BA Ensures the Product Solves the Real Business Need

    Module Progress Reminder

    Your roadmap:

    Total Lessons: 36

    Completed:

    ✅ Lesson 1--29

    Current:

    ➡️ Lesson 30 of 36

    Remaining:

    6 Lessons Left

    1. What is UAT?

    Definition

    User Acceptance Testing (UAT) is the process where business users validate that the developed solution meets their business needs and is ready for real-world use.

    Simple:

    Testing team asks:

    \"Does the system work correctly?\"

    Business users ask:

    \"Does this system solve my problem?\"

    UAT answers:

    \"Can the business accept this product?\"

    2. Where Does UAT Come in Software Lifecycle?

    Requirement

    ↓

    Design

    ↓

    Development

    ↓

    System Testing (QA)

    ↓

    UAT

    ↓

    Business Approval

    ↓

    Production Release

    3. Why is UAT Important?

    A system can pass technical testing but fail business expectations.

    Example:

    Developer builds AI risk dashboard.

    QA confirms:

    ✅ Button works\ ✅ Data loads\ ✅ No errors

    But business discovers:

    ❌ Risk score explanation is missing\ ❌ Workflow does not match reviewer process

    UAT catches these issues.

    4. BA Role in UAT

    The BA is the bridge between:

    Business Users ↔ Development Team

    BA responsibilities:

    1. Plan UAT

    2. Define scenarios

    3. Prepare test data

    4. Coordinate users

    5. Manage defects

    6. Collect approval

    5. UAT Planning Process

    A professional BA follows:

    Understand Business Goal

    ↓

    Identify UAT Participants

    ↓

    Create UAT Strategy

    ↓

    Prepare Test Scenarios

    ↓

    Prepare Test Data

    ↓

    Execute Testing

    ↓

    Record Defects

    ↓

    Business Sign-off

    6. UAT Participants

    Example:

    AI Pharmacovigilance Platform

    User Responsibility

    Safety Reviewer Validate case review process

    Medical Reviewer Validate approval workflow

    Compliance Officer Validate regulatory requirements

    Product Owner Final acceptance

    7. UAT Strategy Document

    A BA may prepare:

    UAT Plan

    Contains:

    1. Objective

    Example:

    \"Validate that AI-assisted case review meets pharmacovigilance team requirements.\"

    2. Scope

    In Scope:

    ✅ Case upload\ ✅ AI summary\ ✅ Risk classification\ ✅ Approval workflow

    Out of Scope:

    ❌ AI model training\ ❌ Database optimization

    3. Test Participants

    Safety reviewers and medical reviewers.

    4. Test Environment

    Example:

    UAT environment with production-like data.

    5. Entry Criteria

    Conditions before UAT starts.

    Example:

    • Development completed

    • QA testing completed

    • Environment ready

    6. Exit Criteria

    Conditions for completion.

    Example:

    • All critical defects closed

    • Business approval received

    8. Creating UAT Test Scenarios

    A BA converts requirements into business scenarios.

    Example User Story:

    As a safety reviewer, I want AI-generated summaries so that I can review cases faster.

    UAT Scenario:

    Scenario:

    Generate AI Case Summary

    Steps:

    1. Login as reviewer

    2. Upload adverse event report

    3. Start AI analysis

    4. View generated summary

    Expected Result:

    System displays:

    • Patient details

    • Drug information

    • Event details

    • Risk information

    9. UAT Test Case Template

    Industry format:

    Field Example

    Test ID UAT-001

    Requirement AI Summary

    Scenario Generate summary

    Preconditions User logged in

    Test Steps Upload report

    Expected Result Summary displayed

    Actual Result Filled during testing

    Status Pass/Fail

    10. UAT Example -- Healthcare AI System

    Feature:

    AI Risk Classification

    Test Case:

    ID:

    UAT-AI-002

    Requirement:

    System should classify safety case risk.

    Steps:

    1. Open completed case.

    2. Select AI analysis.

    3. View risk score.

    Expected:

    System displays:

    • Risk category

    • Confidence score

    • Explanation

    Result:

    Pass / Fail

    11. Negative UAT Scenarios

    Good BA always tests exceptions.

    Example:

    Scenario:

    Invalid document upload

    Steps:

    1. Upload unsupported file

    Expected:

    System displays:

    \"Unsupported format. Please upload PDF or XML.\"

    Other negative cases:

    • Missing mandatory fields

    • Unauthorized user

    • AI processing failure

    • Network interruption

    12. UAT Defect Management

    During UAT defects are found.

    BA helps classify them.

    Defect Severity:

    Severity Meaning

    Critical Business cannot continue

    High Major functionality broken

    Medium Workaround exists

    Low Minor issue

    Example:

    Critical:

    AI gives wrong regulatory classification.

    Low:

    Dashboard alignment issue.

    13. UAT vs QA Testing

    Important interview topic.

    QA Testing UAT

    Technical validation Business validation

    QA team Business users

    Checks defects Checks business acceptance

    System behavior Business outcome

    14. UAT Sign-off

    After successful testing:

    Business provides approval.

    Example:

    \"The AI Pharmacovigilance Platform meets business requirements and is approved for production deployment.\"

    Sign-off usually includes:

    • Date

    • Version tested

    • Approved features

    • Known limitations

    • Stakeholder approval

    15. Common BA Mistakes in UAT

    Mistake 1:

    Starting UAT without clear scenarios.

    Mistake 2:

    Testing only positive cases.

    Mistake 3:

    No business users involved.

    Mistake 4:

    Ignoring defects until release.

    Mistake 5:

    No formal sign-off.

    16. Interview Questions

    Q1. What is UAT?

    Answer:

    \"UAT is business validation testing where end users verify that the solution meets their requirements before production release.\"

    Q2. What is BA\'s role in UAT?

    Answer:

    \"BA prepares UAT scenarios, coordinates users, manages defects, clarifies requirements, and supports business sign-off.\"

    Q3. Difference between QA and UAT?

    Answer:

    \"QA validates whether the system works correctly, while UAT validates whether the system solves the business need.\"

    Lesson 31 -- Defect Management Masterclass

    How a BA Handles Defects from Discovery to Resolution

    Module Progress Reminder

    Your roadmap:

    Total Lessons: 36

    Completed:

    ✅ Lesson 1--30

    Current:

    ➡️ Lesson 31 of 36

    Remaining:

    5 Lessons Left

    1. What is a Defect?

    Definition

    A defect is a gap between the expected behavior of a system and its actual behavior.

    Simple:

    Expected:

    \"The system should show AI risk score.\"

    Actual:

    \"Risk score is missing.\"

    This difference is a defect.

    2. Defect Terminology

    Important BA interview topic.

    Bug

    A coding error that causes incorrect behavior.

    Example:

    Developer calculation mistake.

    Defect

    Any deviation from expected requirement.

    Example:

    Feature does not meet acceptance criteria.

    Issue

    Any problem affecting project delivery.

    Example:

    Missing test data.

    Error

    Human mistake during development.

    Relationship:

    Human Mistake

    ↓

    Error

    ↓

    Creates Bug

    ↓

    Observed as Defect

    ↓

    Reported as Issue

    3. Defect Lifecycle

    A professional defect follows a lifecycle:

    id=\"x3j9cd\"

    New

    ↓

    Assigned

    ↓

    Analysis

    ↓

    Fixed

    ↓

    Retest

    ↓

    Closed

    Detailed:

    1. New

    QA/business identifies problem.

    2. Assigned

    Defect assigned to responsible team.

    3. Analysis

    Developer investigates cause.

    4. Fixed

    Code correction completed.

    5. Retest

    QA verifies fix.

    6. Closed

    Defect confirmed resolved.

    4. Defect Lifecycle Example

    Feature:

    AI Risk Classification

    Problem:

    High-risk case shown as Medium risk.

    Lifecycle:

    New:

    QA reports defect.

    ↓

    Assigned:

    Developer receives issue.

    ↓

    Analysis:

    AI scoring rule incorrect.

    ↓

    Fixed:

    Rule corrected.

    ↓

    Retest:

    QA verifies.

    ↓

    Closed:

    Business accepts.

    5. Severity vs Priority

    Very important interview topic.

    Severity

    Meaning:

    How badly does the defect impact the system?

    Usually decided by:

    QA + Technical team

    Priority

    Meaning:

    How quickly should the defect be fixed?

    Usually decided by:

    Product Owner + Business

    Comparison:

    Severity Priority

    Technical impact Business urgency

    How bad How soon

    QA focus Business focus

    Examples:

    Example 1:

    Critical Severity + High Priority

    AI generates incorrect regulatory decision.

    Impact:

    Patient safety risk.

    Example 2:

    Low Severity + High Priority

    Company logo incorrect before major launch.

    Business importance high.

    Example 3:

    High Severity + Low Priority

    Rare report export failure not used currently.

    6. Defect Classification

    Common categories:

    Functional Defect

    Feature does not work.

    Example:

    Button does not submit case.

    Data Defect

    Incorrect data.

    Example:

    Wrong drug information displayed.

    UI Defect

    Visual issue.

    Example:

    Dashboard alignment problem.

    Performance Defect

    System slow.

    Example:

    AI summary takes 10 minutes.

    Security Defect

    Unauthorized access.

    Example:

    Reviewer can see restricted cases.

    7. BA Role in Defect Management

    BA is not the person fixing bugs.

    BA ensures:

    • Correct understanding

    • Business impact analysis

    • Requirement clarification

    BA Responsibility 1:

    Validate Defect Against Requirement

    Example:

    Defect:

    \"Risk score missing.\"

    BA checks:

    Acceptance Criteria:

    \"Risk score must be displayed.\"

    Confirmed: Valid defect.

    BA Responsibility 2:

    Support Defect Triage

    Defect triage meeting:

    Participants:

    • BA

    • Product Owner

    • Developer

    • QA

    • Scrum Master

    Questions:

    1. Is this really a defect?

    2. How serious is it?

    3. Should it be fixed now?

    4. What is business impact?

    BA Responsibility 3:

    Analyze Business Impact

    Example:

    Defect:

    AI confidence score not visible.

    Impact:

    Reviewers cannot judge AI reliability.

    Decision:

    High priority fix.

    BA Responsibility 4:

    Update Requirements

    Sometimes defects reveal missing requirements.

    Example:

    Problem:

    System does not handle incomplete reports.

    BA adds:

    Business Rule:

    \"System shall notify users when mandatory information is missing.\"

    8. Defect Report Template

    Professional format:

    Field Example

    Defect ID DEF-001

    Title Wrong AI Risk Score

    Description High risk case classified incorrectly

    Environment UAT

    Steps Open case → Run analysis

    Expected High risk

    Actual Medium risk

    Severity Critical

    Priority High

    Status Open

    9. Defect Example -- AI Pharmacovigilance Platform

    Defect:

    AI Risk Classification Error

    Description:

    The system classifies a serious adverse event as low risk.

    Expected:

    High-risk category.

    Actual:

    Low-risk category.

    Business Impact:

    Could delay regulatory review.

    Severity:

    Critical

    Priority:

    High

    Root Cause:

    Incorrect AI classification rule.

    10. Defect Triage Process

    Typical flow:

    Defect Reported

    ↓

    Review Impact

    ↓

    Validate Requirement

    ↓

    Assign Severity/Priority

    ↓

    Decide Fix Timeline

    ↓

    Track Resolution

    11. Defect Management Tools

    Common industry tools:

    • Jira

    • Azure DevOps

    • ServiceNow

    BA uses these tools to:

    • Review defects

    • Add clarification

    • Link defects to stories

    • Track status

    12. Defect Metrics

    Agile teams track:

    Defect Count

    Number of defects found.

    Defect Leakage

    Defects escaping into production.

    Formula:

    Production Defects /

    Total Defects

    Defect Resolution Time

    How quickly defects are fixed.

    Reopen Rate

    How many defects return after fixing.

    13. Root Cause Analysis of Defects

    Not every defect should only be fixed.

    BA asks:

    \"Why did this happen?\"

    Example:

    Problem:

    Wrong risk score.

    Why?

    ↓

    AI rule incorrect.

    Why?

    ↓

    Requirement did not define classification rules.

    Why?

    ↓

    Business rules missing.

    Root Cause:

    Incomplete requirement analysis.

    14. Common BA Mistakes

    Mistake 1:

    Accepting every reported problem as defect.

    Mistake 2:

    Ignoring business impact.

    Mistake 3:

    No requirement traceability.

    Mistake 4:

    Fixing symptom instead of root cause.

    15. Interview Questions

    Q1. Difference between severity and priority?

    Answer:

    \"Severity measures impact of the defect, while priority determines how quickly it should be resolved.\"

    Q2. What is BA\'s role in defect management?

    Answer:

    \"BA validates defects against requirements, analyzes business impact, supports triage, and ensures correct resolution.\"

    Q3. What is defect leakage?

    Answer:

    \"Defect leakage occurs when defects are missed during testing and reach production.\"

    Practical Assignment

    For AI Pharmacovigilance Platform:

    Create 5 defects.

    For each:

    1. Defect Title

    2. Description

    3. Expected Result

    4. Actual Result

    5. Severity

    6. Priority

    7. Business Impact

    How a BA Supports Product Release, Deployment & Business Transition

    Module Progress Reminder

    1. What is Release Documentation?

    Definition

    Release Documentation is a set of documents that describe what is being released, what has changed, how users should use it, and how the organization should transition to the new version.

    Simple:

    Release documentation answers:

    • What are we releasing?

    • Why are we releasing it?

    • What changed?

    • Who is impacted?

    • How should users use it?

    2. Where Does Release Documentation Come?

    Software lifecycle:

    Requirement

    ↓

    Development

    ↓

    Testing

    ↓

    UAT

    ↓

    Release Preparation

    ↓

    Production Deployment

    ↓

    Post Release Support

    Release documentation happens before production launch.

    3. Why is Release Documentation Important?

    A good release document provides:

    ✅ Clear communication\ ✅ User awareness\ ✅ Deployment readiness\ ✅ Compliance evidence\ ✅ Change tracking

    Especially important in:

    • Healthcare

    • Banking

    • Government

    • Regulated industries

    4. BA Role in Release Management

    A BA supports:

    1. Release planning

    2. Requirement verification

    3. Business communication

    4. User documentation

    5. Training support

    6. Go-live readiness

    BA does not usually deploy software.

    Technical teams handle deployment.

    5. Types of Release Documents

    A BA may create or support:

    1. Release Notes

    Most common.

    Explains:

    • New features

    • Improvements

    • Bug fixes

    • Known limitations

    2. User Guide

    Explains:

    • How users operate the system

    3. Training Material

    Supports adoption.

    4. Deployment Checklist

    Ensures readiness.

    5. Change Request Documentation

    Tracks approved changes.

    6. Release Plan

    Defines:

    • Timeline

    • Activities

    • Responsibilities

    6. Release Notes Structure

    Professional format:

    Release Version:

    Release Date:

    Overview:

    New Features:

    Enhancements:

    Bug Fixes:

    Known Issues:

    Impact:

    7. Release Notes Example

    Product:

    AI Pharmacovigilance Platform

    Version:

    Release 2.0

    Overview:

    This release introduces AI-assisted safety case analysis capabilities.

    New Features:

    1. AI Case Summary

    Users can generate automated summaries from safety reports.

    2. Risk Classification

    System provides AI-based risk categories.

    3. Reviewer Dashboard

    Users can view prioritized cases.

    Improvements:

    • Faster document processing

    • Improved search capability

    • Better case filtering

    Bug Fixes:

    • Fixed duplicate case display issue

    • Fixed report export error

    Known Limitations:

    • AI recommendations require human validation.

    8. Release Readiness Checklist

    Before go-live:

    Business Readiness

    ✅ UAT completed\ ✅ Business approval received\ ✅ Users informed

    Functional Readiness

    ✅ Features tested\ ✅ Defects resolved\ ✅ Acceptance criteria completed

    Technical Readiness

    ✅ Deployment completed\ ✅ Backup available\ ✅ Monitoring enabled

    Documentation Readiness

    ✅ User guide ready\ ✅ Release notes published\ ✅ Training completed

    9. Go-Live Checklist Example

    AI Pharmacovigilance Platform

    Before launch:

    Activity Status

    UAT sign-off Complete

    Security validation Complete

    User training Complete

    Data migration Complete

    Production deployment Planned

    10. Change Management in Releases

    A release often changes user behavior.

    Example:

    Old Process:

    Reviewer manually reads reports.

    ↓

    New Process:

    AI generates summary first.

    BA helps manage:

    • Communication

    • Training

    • Adoption issues

    11. Version Management

    Software releases use versions.

    Example:

    Major Release

    2.0

    Large new capabilities.

    Minor Release

    2.1

    Enhancements.

    Patch Release

    2.1.1

    Bug fixes.

    Example:

    AI Platform:

    Version 1.0:

    Basic case management

    Version 2.0:

    AI analysis added

    Version 2.0.1:

    Bug fixes

    12. BA Role During Go-Live

    During release:

    BA supports:

    1. Business Validation

    Confirm:

    \"Does released feature match expectation?\"

    2. User Support

    Answer:

    • How does feature work?

    • What changed?

    3. Issue Analysis

    If users report problems:

    BA helps classify:

    • Requirement issue

    • Training issue

    • Defect

    13. Post Release Activities

    After launch:

    BA helps collect:

    • User feedback

    • Improvement requests

    • New requirements

    Example:

    Users request:

    \"Add AI explanation history.\"

    BA converts:

    Feedback →

    Feature Request →

    User Story

    Answer:

    1. Why Portfolio Projects Matter for a BA?

    A BA interview is not only about theory.

    Companies ask:

    \"Tell me about a project where you analyzed requirements, worked with stakeholders, and delivered a solution.\"

    You need a realistic project story.

    For your target path:

    Pharmacologist → Healthcare AI BA/Product Analyst

    A healthcare AI project is ideal because it combines:

    • Domain knowledge

    • Business analysis

    • AI product understanding

    • Agile execution

    2. Project Case Study Introduction

    Project Name:

    AI Pharmacovigilance Intelligence Platform

    Industry:

    Pharmaceutical / Life Sciences

    Business Area:

    Drug Safety Monitoring

    Project Type:

    AI-powered SaaS platform

    3. Business Background

    A pharmaceutical company receives thousands of adverse event reports from:

    • Patients

    • Doctors

    • Hospitals

    • Regulatory authorities

    Currently, safety teams manually:

    • Read reports

    • Extract information

    • Classify severity

    • Prioritize cases

    • Prepare regulatory submissions

    4. Existing Business Problem (AS-IS)

    Current process:

    Adverse Event Received

    ↓

    Manual Data Entry

    ↓

    Safety Reviewer Analysis

    ↓

    Medical Review

    ↓

    Regulatory Submission

    Problems:

    Problem 1:

    Large manual workload.

    Example:

    Reviewer spends 20--30 minutes per case.

    Problem 2:

    Delayed case processing.

    Problem 3:

    High chance of inconsistency.

    Problem 4:

    Difficulty identifying high-risk cases quickly.

    5. BA Problem Statement

    Professional BA format:

    Current Situation:

    The pharmacovigilance team manually processes adverse event reports.

    Business Problem:

    Manual review causes delays and increases operational effort.

    Business Impact:

    • Higher operational cost

    • Delayed safety reporting

    • Increased reviewer workload

    Business Need:

    Implement AI-assisted case processing.

    6. Stakeholder Analysis

    A BA begins with stakeholders.

    Stakeholder Role Interest

    Head of Pharmacovigilance Business Owner Reduce review time

    Safety Reviewer End User Faster case analysis

    Medical Reviewer Validator Accurate assessment

    Compliance Team Regulatory Ensure compliance

    IT Team Technical System delivery

    Product Manager Product Direction Roadmap

    7. Stakeholder Power-Interest Matrix

    High Power / High Interest

    Manage closely:

    • Head of Pharmacovigilance

    • Product Owner

    High Power / Low Interest

    Keep satisfied:

    • Compliance leadership

    Low Power / High Interest

    Keep informed:

    • Safety reviewers

    Low Power / Low Interest

    Monitor:

    • Supporting teams

    8. Business Objectives

    The BA defines measurable goals.

    Objective 1:

    Reduce manual case review time by 50%.

    Objective 2:

    Improve case prioritization accuracy.

    Objective 3:

    Provide audit-ready review history.

    Objective 4:

    Improve regulatory reporting efficiency.

    9. Scope Definition

    In Scope

    ✅ AI document extraction\ ✅ Case summarization\ ✅ Risk classification\ ✅ Reviewer dashboard\ ✅ Audit trail

    Out of Scope

    ❌ Drug discovery\ ❌ Patient diagnosis\ ❌ Treatment recommendation

    ↓

    Regulatory Reporting

    Step Issue

    ↓

    Human Validation

    ↓

    Regulatory Submission

    AI does not replace humans.

    It supports decision-making.

    The company requires an AI-powered pharmacovigilance platform to improve safety case processing efficiency.

    Success Metrics:

    Metric Target

    User adoption >90%

    Lesson 34 -- Pharmacovigilance System Analysis

    Complete Safety System Workflow Analysis for a Healthcare BA

    Module Progress Reminder

    Your roadmap:

    Total Lessons: 36

    Completed:

    ✅ Lesson 1--33

    Current:

    ➡️ Lesson 34 of 36

    Remaining:

    2 Lessons Left

    1. Why Pharmacovigilance Domain Knowledge Matters for a BA?

    A general BA understands:

    • Requirements

    • Processes

    • Stakeholders

    • Systems

    A Healthcare BA must additionally understand:

    • Medical workflows

    • Regulatory expectations

    • Safety processes

    • Industry terminology

    Your advantage:

    You already understand pharmacology, so you can connect:

    Clinical Domain → Business Need → Software Requirement

    2. What is Pharmacovigilance (PV)?

    Definition

    Pharmacovigilance is the science and activities related to detecting, assessing, understanding, and preventing adverse effects or other medicine-related problems.

    Simple:

    PV ensures:

    \"Medicines remain safe after reaching patients.\"

    3. Pharmacovigilance System Overview

    A typical PV ecosystem:

    Patient / Doctor / Hospital

    ↓

    Adverse Event Report

    ↓

    Safety Database

    ↓

    Case Processing

    ↓

    Medical Review

    ↓

    Signal Detection

    ↓

    Regulatory Reporting

    4. PV System Stakeholders

    1. Patient

    Provides safety information.

    Example:

    \"I experienced nausea after taking Drug X.\"

    2. Healthcare Professional

    Reports adverse events.

    3. Drug Safety Associate

    Processes cases.

    4. Medical Reviewer

    Evaluates clinical significance.

    5. Pharmacovigilance Manager

    Ensures compliance.

    6. Regulatory Authority

    Receives safety reports.

    Examples:

    • FDA

    • EMA

    • CDSCO

    5. Adverse Event Case Lifecycle

    This is very important for BA interviews.

    Complete lifecycle:

    Case Intake

    ↓

    Case Creation

    ↓

    Data Entry

    ↓

    Medical Coding

    ↓

    Assessment

    ↓

    Quality Review

    ↓

    Submission

    ↓

    Follow-up

    ↓

    Closure

    Let\'s understand each stage.

    6. Stage 1 -- Case Intake

    What happens?

    Safety information enters the system.

    Sources:

    • Email

    • Call center

    • Website

    • Literature

    • Regulatory authority

    BA Requirements:

    System should support multiple intake channels.

    Example Requirement:

    FR-001:

    \"The system shall allow users to create safety cases from incoming reports.\"

    7. Stage 2 -- Case Creation

    A case record is created.

    Minimum information:

    Four Minimum Criteria:

    1. Identifiable patient

    2. Identifiable reporter

    3. Suspect product

    4. Adverse event

    BA Business Rule:

    System shall not allow case validation without minimum criteria.

    8. Stage 3 -- Data Entry

    Information captured:

    Patient Information

    • Age

    • Gender

    • Medical history

    Drug Information

    • Product name

    • Dose

    • Route

    • Batch number

    Event Information

    • Symptoms

    • Seriousness

    • Outcome

    BA Consideration:

    Which fields are mandatory?

    Which fields are optional?

    9. Stage 4 -- Medical Coding

    Raw medical terms are converted into standardized terminology.

    Examples:

    Patient says:

    \"Heart attack\"

    System coding:

    \"Myocardial infarction\"

    BA Requirements:

    System should support controlled medical dictionaries.

    10. Stage 5 -- Medical Assessment

    Medical reviewer evaluates:

    • Seriousness

    • Severity

    • Causality

    • Expectedness

    Example:

    Question:

    \"Is Drug X responsible for liver injury?\"

    BA Requirement:

    System shall allow medical reviewers to record assessment decisions.

    11. Stage 6 -- Quality Review

    Quality team checks:

    • Completeness

    • Accuracy

    • Compliance

    BA Requirement:

    System shall support reviewer approval workflow.

    12. Stage 7 -- Regulatory Submission

    Safety reports may be submitted to authorities.

    Examples:

    • Individual Case Safety Reports (ICSR)

    BA Requirement:

    System shall generate regulatory-compliant reports.

    13. Stage 8 -- Follow-up

    Additional information may arrive.

    Example:

    Initial report:

    Patient experienced reaction.

    Follow-up:

    Lab results received.

    BA Requirement:

    System shall maintain complete case history.

    14. Stage 9 -- Case Closure

    Case is completed.

    System records:

    • Final assessment

    • Submission status

    • Approval history

    15. PV System Modules

    A modern PV platform may contain:

    1. Case Management Module

    Handles:

    • Case creation

    • Workflow

    • Tracking

    2. Document Management

    Stores:

    • Reports

    • Attachments

    • Evidence

    3. Medical Review Module

    Handles:

    • Assessment

    • Decisions

    4. Reporting Module

    Creates:

    • Regulatory reports

    • Dashboards

    5. Signal Detection Module

    Identifies:

    Potential safety concerns.

    6. AI Assistance Module

    Provides:

    • Data extraction

    • Summary generation

    • Risk prediction

    16. BA Requirement Examples for PV System

    Functional Requirements

    FR-001

    System shall allow safety users to create adverse event cases.

    FR-002

    System shall maintain complete case history.

    FR-003

    System shall support medical assessment workflow.

    FR-004

    System shall generate regulatory reports.

    FR-005

    System shall maintain audit trails.

    17. Non-Functional Requirements

    Security

    \"Only authorized users shall access patient safety information.\"

    Performance

    \"System shall load case details within 3 seconds.\"

    Auditability

    \"System shall record all case modifications.\"

    Availability

    \"System shall maintain 99.5% uptime.\"

    18. PV AI Use Cases

    Modern AI applications:

    1. Intelligent Case Intake

    AI extracts:

    • Drug names

    • Events

    • Patient details

    2. Duplicate Case Detection

    AI identifies:

    Same patient + same event + same drug.

    3. Medical Summary Generation

    AI creates reviewer summaries.

    4. Risk Prioritization

    AI identifies:

    High-risk cases.

    5. Literature Screening

    AI analyzes scientific publications.

    19. BA Process Modeling Example

    AS-IS

    Report Received

    ↓

    Manual Entry

    ↓

    Manual Review

    ↓

    Manual Priority Decision

    ↓

    Submission

    Problems:

    • Slow

    • Labour intensive

    • Error possibility

    TO-BE

    Report Received

    ↓

    AI Extraction

    ↓

    Automatic Case Creation

    ↓

    AI Risk Score

    ↓

    Human Validation

    ↓

    Submission

    Benefits:

    • Faster processing

    • Better consistency

    • Reduced workload

    For this PV project, a BA can create:

    ✅ FRD\ ✅ User Stories\ ✅ Acceptance Criteria\ ✅ NFR Document

    Agile Documents

    ✅ Product Backlog\ ✅ Sprint Stories\ ✅ UAT Plan\ ✅ Release Notes

    Lesson 35 -- Digital Health Product Requirements Masterclass

    How a BA Analyzes and Defines Requirements for Digital Health Products

    Module Progress Reminder

    Your roadmap:

    Total Lessons: 36

    ✅ Lesson 1--34
